## October 10 shared-client clipping hypothesis; guest exclusive ownership capability verified

Independent research reviewed the failed256 cap: retain disabled, do not tighten128. [Apple IOAudioStream source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/apple-oss-distributions/IOAudioFamily/IOAudioFamily-144.2.1/IOAudioStream.cpp) makes shared-client gating a plausible downstream mechanism: output clients update ordered mixed positions, and clipping is limited by the earliest client's progress. An early reference IOProc return therefore need not mean combined samples have reached DMA. This is a hypothesis for the installed guest stack, not proof or an emulator playback patch.

First candidate was supported Warcraft audio-system disable. Diagnostic-only31930 inspected actual1.00 Sound Options: effects/music checkboxes unchecked, sliders High/~40%, ambient/movement/unit responses checked, Environmental Effects checked,3D positional off. Physical Apple02DBDMA engine still active with `IOAudioEngineNumActiveUserClients=1`/State1. No separate sound-hardware disconnect option shown. No known references collected, setup **unscored** and stopped. Binary string attempt could not resolve the executable path via the legacy utility; no inferred switches/config edits. Do not repeat failed blind clicks or call this a negative sound-disabled control.

Compile command uses gcc, `-std=gnu99 -Wall -Wextra -Werror -mmacosx-version-min=10.4 -isysroot /Developer/SDKs/MacOSX10.4u.sdk -framework CoreAudio`. Initial missing min-version failed linkage; corrected compile succeeds. Query/lease never run on physical host audio devices.

## October 10 bounded physical-slot observer: clean unloaded controls; short descriptors limit lookahead

Implemented opt-in `POWEREMU_SCREAMER_SLOT_CAPTURE` in `hw/audio/screamer-slot.h`, included from Screamer. This is **diagnostics only; playback is unchanged**. Revised slots contain 8 frames with 8/16-frame lookahead, respecting actual bounds. This principally tests post-read late filling; its sub-millisecond initial lead cannot exclude an erase before the first PRE observation. Do not assume physical continuity across DBDMA descriptors to obtain longer lookahead.

The research agent identified a measurement defect: the existing opt-in PCM recorder drains to disk synchronously on the main/emulation thread every 100 ms. ## Original October 8 conclusion (historical)

A confirmed Screamer timing bug is fixed. In matched live Tiger / Radeon 9800 / four-CPU / 1.67 GHz reported-speed tests, the unchanged pacer inserted 488 short silence gaps into a steady tone. With the fix, the tone has zero such gaps in its 39-second interior. This is PCM handed to CoreAudio, not a recording of the physical speakers. **Warcraft and Chess are not yet validated with the fix, and audio is not declared fully fixed.**

The steady tone also showed 949 sample-to-sample jumps above 1.25× the theoretical sine-wave step bound before the fix, and zero afterward. Peak amplitude was unchanged. See `/tmp/pe9800/oct08-pacer-tone-edge-comparison.json`; this excludes onset/tail and is specific to the 1 kHz tone.

The Mac locked during testing, preventing further GUI interaction. No attempt was made to bypass the lock. CLI audio tests continue in an isolated guest. The original Tiger disk is the read-only backing of the test overlay and its original VM remains stopped; never start that original guest while these tests run.

## Confirmed cause and fix

In `hw/audio/screamer.c`, `screamer_pace_cb` calculates fractional sample allowance using elapsed virtual time. A dispatched QEMU timer is no longer pending. Completing a DBDMA descriptor synchronously invokes `dbdma_end`, then `channel_run`, then `pmac_screamer_tx` for the next descriptor. The TX entry point treats a nonpending pacer as an idle clock and resets `pace_frac` to zero. The reset silently discards a fraction of a frame on many ticks.

Arm the next pacer tick **before transferring DMA**, so synchronous descriptor continuation sees the clock as running. Remove the old end-of-callback arm. Explicitly delete the prearmed timer on the existing idle-stop branch. Guest sample rate, clock ownership, 1 ms pacing interval, DMA contents and BQL ownership are unchanged.

`tests/poweremu/screamer-pacing-test.py` extracts the actual pacer and TX/RX entry points and reproduces synchronous descriptor chaining with QEMU's timer-dispatch semantics. The same pacer now distinguishes host backpressure from DMA inactivity: pending TX/RX work keeps its timer armed even when no frames move, so a full host queue cannot permanently strand a descriptor. A 500 ms full-queue/recovery regression passes; genuine DMA idle still stops after 200 ticks.

Exact frame conservation passes over 10,000 irregular ticks at 22.05, 32, 44.1 and 48 kHz, TX-only and duplex, idle stop/restart, with ASan/UBSan. Saved pre-fix source fails the conservation assertion.

## Live results

| Test | Interior silence runs ≥ 8 frames | Longest gap | Notes |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Old pacer, SPSC | 488 | 116 frames | About 39.5 seconds; periodic backend shortages of roughly 443–651 frames/second |
| Fixed pacer, SPSC | 0 | 0 | About 39 seconds; no steady-state backend shortages |
| Fixed pacer, four competing guest CPU workers | 2 | 3402 frames | One 63.789 ms servicing stall; 1024 missing host frames followed by 3402 exact-zero frames already in guest DMA |

The loaded run also contains earlier speech and natural speech silence. Analyze the tone's last 45 seconds, excluding the first/last three seconds; do not classify all zeros in the combined recording as faults. The gap during CPU load remains a separate issue, despite elimination of the frequent fractional-budget gaps.

## Optional experiments retained

1. `POWEREMU_COREAUDIO_SPSC=1`: CoreAudio callback consumes a single-producer/single-consumer PCM ring without mutexes or BQL; unset/0 retains legacy path. ASan/UBSan and TSan pass 200,000 ordered stereo frames, wrap, variable callback sizes, tail zero fill, old device and disabled buffer cases. No production default changed.
2. `POWEREMU_COREAUDIO_CAPTURE=/absolute/prefix`: callback pushes actual driver-input float32 stereo PCM into a separate four-second diagnostic ring; main thread drains to files named `prefix.PID.GENERATION-RATE.f32`. No disk IO in callback. Check `Diagnostic PCM capture dropped 0 bytes` after backend stop. This diagnostics path remains opt-in.
3. A Darwin main-loop interactive-QoS prototype compiled, but its playback runs were invalid because the host stopped delivering callbacks. The prototype was removed from production source; it is saved at `/tmp/pe9800/main-qos-experiment.c`. Do not claim a scheduling benefit or regression from silent captures. Keep BQL serialization intact.
4. `POWEREMU_AUDIO_TIMING=1`: pre-existing main-loop diagnostics distinguish polling lateness from waiting for BQL.

No adaptive resampler is implemented. The confirmed periodic shortage was a lost fractional allowance, not proven host-clock drift. A resampler cannot repair long stretches of zero samples already present in guest DMA.

## Additional confirmed code defects corrected

- CoreAudio `DeviceIsRunning` is a device-wide state shared by all clients. It is not proof that this PowerEmu IOProc has started. Output now tracks its own `ioproc_started` state, starts its callback even if another client uses the device, makes repeated enable/disable idempotent, retries failed starts/stops, and resets state when replacing/destroying the device. `tests/poweremu/coreaudio-lifecycle-test.py` passes under ASan/UBSan; the saved old source fails the already-running-device assertion. This is a confirmed logic error, **not a proven explanation for the later blank live recordings**.
- The legacy callback now respects the actual supplied `mDataByteSize`, rather than a period cached during initialization. Smaller buffers preserve remaining PCM without writing past their end; larger buffers receive available samples followed by silence. Null/empty output storage and obsolete-device buffers are handled without stale output. The callback fixture now checks smaller/larger legacy periods too; the saved old source fails the smaller-period assertion. SPSC remains optional, not a shipping default.
- Additional debug counters distinguish an IOProc never invoked from an invocation with unusable output storage. Start/stop results are logged only from the main thread when diagnostics are enabled.
- `tests/poweremu/coreaudio-capture-test.py` checks production diagnostic capture helpers for full/overflow/wrap, concurrent byte order, exact drop accounting and cleanup. ASan/UBSan and TSan pass.

## Host-output blocker after the Mac locked

Later QoS-on AND QoS-off runs produced empty files. A diagnostic backend explicitly logged `AudioDeviceStart` success (`device 108 status 0`), with **zero IOProc invocations**, not merely empty PCM buffers. A separate tiny native CoreAudio program outside QEMU reproduced successful create/start with zero callbacks over ten seconds on the MacBook Air speakers. Repeating at 48 kHz also yielded zero callbacks; its temporary nominal-rate change was restored successfully to 44.1 kHz.

Therefore the later silence cannot establish guest audio quality, SPSC benefit, or QoS benefit. Do not call an empty capture a passing run. The exact cause of the host callback suppression is still unknown; its timing after host lock is correlation, not proof. Do not bypass the lock or restart the system audio service without assessing other apps. The probes are `/tmp/pe9800/oct08-host-audio-probe{,48}.c`, executables and matching `.log` files. The original native 488-vs-zero comparison remains valid and contains nonzero tone PCM.

Two headless runs per path BEFORE callback suppression were valid: legacy had 3 interior gaps on run 1 and zero on run 2; SPSC had 3 on run 1 and 2 on run 2. Some stalls included >200 ms combined polling/BQL waits. They reinforce that SPSC alone does not solve guest production/service stalls. See `/tmp/pe9800/oct08-headless-ab-analysis.json`; each measurement excludes tone startup/tail and the natural silence between runs.

## Current testing and next steps

`/tmp/pe9800/oct08-audio-bench.py` boots the same isolated overlay headlessly with the current backend, four actual CPUs, same 9800 and resolution, persistent clock tools, and a private host clock server. It replays the native backend arguments, removes the native display IPC object, and uses only SSH plus sound/CPU test helpers. This differs from native-window presentation; do not mistake it for live Warcraft validation.

First confirm the standalone host probe receives callbacks and a tone recording contains nonzero samples. Then run matched legacy/SPSC tests. Restore the QoS prototype only as a separate experiment if useful. Each loaded tone lasts 45 seconds with four guest CPU workers; workers are killed through a trap using only their recorded PIDs. Inspect main-loop and Screamer timing, capture-drop counters and actual PCM. Repeat before promoting an option; one clean run is not proof that rare stalls are gone.

After the Mac is unlocked:

1. Use CUA to launch Warcraft's no-CD desktop app and load a real map. Capture at least several minutes with movement, unit speech, music, and clean quit. Do not use monitor-injected mouse/keyboard or AppleScript.
2. Test multiple Chess computer-move announcements, including idle-to-speech onset, and preserve graphics correctness.
3. Compare the 9200 in the same binary and matched scene. Verify native-window performance and audio, then output-device switch and VM pause/resume.
4. Package a user-facing test build with the validated settings; remove test-library overrides and PCM diagnostics from that build. Keep the original test overlay isolated. Do not commit, release or enable unvalidated defaults.

## Follow-up: Warcraft freezes after pops improved

User reports far fewer pops, almost perfect audio, but occasional roughly half-second Warcraft/emulator freezes. This is user-observed improvement, not proof all game/audio regressions are resolved. Overnight automation was paused when the user returned. Host standalone probe now receives callbacks (862 in ten seconds), so live audio validation can resume.

A concrete cache cliff was found in `hw/display/ppc_mac_gpu_r300_metal.m`: shader-source and Metal-pipeline caches discarded **all** entries upon reaching 64. New programs can therefore trigger renewed synchronous compilation of previously hot pipelines while graphics owns BQL. This is a plausible freeze contributor, **not yet a measured explanation of the user's current pauses**.

Changed both caches to bounded 64-entry LRU caches. New keys evict only the least recently used entry; hits retain the stored immutable key. Exact shader/pipeline keys, locking, submission order and audio settings remain unchanged. Shared compute export pipelines use the same bounded cache. No BQL unlock experiment was introduced.

`tests/poweremu/r300-cache-test.py` extracts the production cache, checks 63 hot entries surviving 1,000 cold insertions, bounded capacity, replacement, copied mutable keys and mutable lookup keys, under ASan/UBSan. PASS. Full existing R300 Metal pixel/rejection suite PASS on Apple M5. Backend rebuilt successfully.

Signed separate candidate: `/Users/adam/Developer/PowerEmu/build/PowerEmu Freeze Test.app`. It uses the normal VM library and unchanged audio defaults. App-specific `LSEnvironment` enables `POWEREMU_AUDIO_TIMING=1` and `SCREAMER_DEBUG=1`; original audio candidate remains intact. Root deep/strict signature verification passes. Logs: `/tmp/pe9800/oct08-lru-metal-test.log`, `oct08-lru-backend-build.log`, `oct08-freeze-app-build.log`. Pre-change source snapshot: `/tmp/pe9800/oct08-r300-metal-before-lru.m`.

Next gameplay test: quit the previous frontend, launch Freeze Test and use the same Warcraft map/CPU/card settings. Preserve VM `Logs/gpu-trace.log` and backend output afterward. `R350_PIPELINE_BUILD us=... cached=... shader=...` records successful pipeline builds taking >=5ms in backend output; correlate with main-loop BQL wait intervals and GPU ring intervals in gpu-trace. If freezes occur without compilation, investigate the measured lock holder/GPU fence or host scheduling rather than assuming the cache fixed them. No post-change Warcraft gameplay result yet. Do not commit or release.

Continuous background audio can keep the host IOProc running even as a new game sound is decoded, so new-sound onset alone does not prove host restart is responsible. Need timestamped transition durations and actual PCM/event correlation before changing lifecycle behavior.

### October 8: confirmed network-relay stall and Freeze Test 3

The user shut down the original Tiger guest after reporting new-sound breakup in Freeze Test 2. Finished logs are preserved in `/tmp/pe9800/oct08-freeze2-completed`. Original guest is now stopped. **The earlier isolated overlay's backing image became stale after the user's original-disk runs; do not reuse it.** New `/tmp/pe9800/oct08-onset-isolated.qcow2` was created against the clean, stopped original Tiger image, and all current benches use it. Never open that backing image writable while this overlay is running.

A loaded, continuous Sound Manager tone reproduced long gaps without Warcraft or ongoing GPU drawing. Temporary BQL and MMIO attribution identified a 161.734 ms hold inside `sungem.txdma` (CPU0, MMIO address0/value71), corresponding to a 161.944 ms BQL hold. Source inspection showed libslirp's `cmd:` forward creates a relay with GLib spawning/child setup during the network operation. This is a measured network stall plus an A/B-confirmed trigger; it does not attribute every Warcraft sound-onset pause to networking. Host AudioDeviceStart/Stop took approximately27–40ms at activation/shutdown, but were absent at the reproduced mid-tone network pauses. A scheduling/QoS experiment did not eliminate gaps and was removed.

**Implemented:** `net/slirp.c` supports `guestfwd=tcp:ADDRESS:PORT-unix-connect:PATH` through the existing `slirp_add_unix` API. Libslirp opens a separate host Unix socket for every guest TCP connection. No process launch, no shared chardev stream, no library fork or patched Homebrew dependency. Original `unix:` character-device syntax and `cmd:` behavior are preserved. Non-Unix hosts reject this new forwarding mode. `qemu-options.hx` documents it.

`VMRunner.swift` selects this new path for all nine service forwards in the experimental comparison app: agent7700, WebDAV80, clock7701, IMAP143, SMTP25/587, web7780, music3001, printing515. Each connection remains independent. Ordinary bundles still use the existing relay because their older backend does not understand the extension. This selection uses the existing experimental-bundle marker and applies to both selectable9200 and9800 cards. Before promoting into ordinary releases, update the shipping backend/capability selection together. No guest-tools reinstall required.

Validation:

- Actual host callback PCM, stereo44.1kHz, four active guest CPUs with four CPU-load workers and45-second tones. Ignore first/last3seconds of each active interval; no empty recordings accepted. Captures report **zero dropped bytes**.

- First Unix-forward run:0silent gaps across~39s; second run had2,737silent frames across3shorter gaps (longest1,185frames/~27ms). Additional clock clients were exercised during that experiment; do not call it a clean perfect-audio result.
- Removed all temporary CPU/main-loop/MMIO probes and QoS changes, then repeated with the same final backend/audio settings: **two Unix-forward runs each had0silent gaps across~39s**. Same-backend `cmd:` control reproduced12,468silent frames across2gaps (~283ms total; longest7,359frames/~167ms). These are synthetic loaded-tone results, not a Warcraft gameplay verdict.
- Eight concurrent guest clock probes all printed valid time responses. The shell's PID-wait bookkeeping reported an error, so these response logs do not establish eight clean child exit statuses. Evidence: `/tmp/pe9800/oct08-unix-clock-concurrent.log` and preserved guest response output. Underlying library source confirms separate sockets; further real service/reconnect testing remains useful.
- `tests/poweremu/guestfwd-unix-test.py` compiles the actual QEMU parser with mocked backend calls under ASan/UBSan: correct full path including spaces, address/port/empty-path rejection, backend registration failure, and preservation of legacy char/cmd dispatch all pass. - Existing CoreAudio lifecycle/output and Screamer pacing fixtures pass. Backend and Swift app builds pass. Existing Metal cache/render changes remain intact. No global BQL instrumentation or scheduling experiment remains in source.
